17-21% On-demand AI GPU
pricing hike to shift value
from LLM companies to cloud
vendors
Nebius 10/1 全⾯調漲  Nvidia AI GPU ， CPU ， Memory 租⽤價
格，我們認為短期市場價值從賣  tokens 的⼤語⾔ / 專家語⾔模型公司
轉到  AI 數據中⼼算⼒基礎建設公司
ANDREW LU ON GLOBAL SEMI/TECHS

READ IN APP
根據路透社  （Nebius hikes AI cloud prices again as demand for
computing power soars）， Euronext ， MarketBeat 9 ⽉  17 ⽇報導，
Nebius Group 宣布⾃  2026 年  10 ⽉  1 ⽇起局部調升  On-demand 算⼒租
⾦：其中  Nvidia H100 租⽤價格由每⼩時  US$3.85 調升  16.9% ⾄
US$4.50 ；次世代旗艦  B300 算⼒租⾦更⼤漲  21% 從每⼩時 US$7.85 調升
到  US$9.50 。除此以外， Nebius 也調⾼部分 AMD/Intel x86 CPU 租⽤價格
達 25% ，及存儲記憶體租⽤價格達 41% ， Nebius 同步確認其簽約容量全速朝
向年底  5 GW ⽬標邁進。同⽇， CoreWeave 官⽅宣布⾃  6 ⽉底以來持續以
更⾼價格簽約算⼒，其第三季度簽訂的  3 ⾄  6 個⽉短期客戶合約，年化定價

達到每  MW 約  4,000 萬美元（折合  1 GW 算⼒租⽤合約每年⾼達  400 億美
元），合約總⽤電容量⾃  3.7 GW 增⾄  4.2 GW 。為⽀撐龐⼤建置，
CoreWeave 同步啟動  30 億美元可轉換優先票據發售（另附  5 億美元超額配
售權），並設⽴⾼達  3,500 萬股（約  29.2 億美元）的市價增發（ ATM ）融資
機制。

資⾦鏈脆弱的⼆線  AI 模型訓練新創：短期突發 AI GPU 算⼒租⾦價格⼤漲達
17-21% ， x86 CPU 租⽤價格跳升達 25% ，存儲記憶體租⽤價格飆升達
41% ，每 GW 算⼒年化達  400 億美元，⼤幅拉⾼訓練中繼或追加推論負載的邊
際成本，現⾦消耗速度失控，對於⼀線⼤語⾔模型公司  Anthropic 及
OpenAI 還有龐⼤付費訂閱及 Tokens 銷售營收 ARR 可以⽀撐，但對於缺乏龐
⼤訂閱⽤戶及 Tokens 銷售的⼆線⼤語⾔及專家模型新創公司就相當⾟苦，尤
其是每百萬 Tokens 價格受到中國⼤陸開源模型競爭⽽持續下滑。
我們之前常將  Oracle 及  CoreWeave 龐⼤負債視為「流動性吃緊的危險信
號」 ( 請參考淨負債對股東權益⽐較下⽅表格）。⾮共識深度洞察在於，這是外
溢雲  Neocloud 巨頭在主動實施以龐⼤算⼒租⽤在⼿訂單需求  ( 請參考下圖，

Till 2Q26 各⼤ AI 算⼒租⽤數據中⼼在⼿訂單是未來 4 年預測營收加總的 10 倍
左右）的強⼤⽀撐來反應 AI 算⼒數據中⼼基礎建設，電⼒及晶⽚價格價格的飆
漲。短期  3-6 個⽉合約能賣到每  MW 年化  4,000 萬美元，代表市場極度缺乏
能夠「⽴即通電」的即戰⼒算⼒。 AI 算⼒數據中⼼趁合約定價處於歷史⾼檔，
以可轉債鎖定低成本資⾦，是在為  2027 年可能出現的電⼒擴建壁壘建⽴不可
跨越的護城河。

### Page 1

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 9 月 7 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 全球公債殖利率飆高 ， 聯準會理事緩和升息預期 ：通膨疑慮及政府債務問題推升長天期公債殖
利率。近期日本、美國、英國、德國及法國長天期公債殖利率升至多年高點； Fed 理事
Christopher Waller 表示，若通膨持續朝 2% 目標邁進，他傾向在 9 月會議按兵不動，並強
調 9 月決策將高度依賴即將公布的 8 月 CPI 數據 ， 市場解讀此為相對鴿派訊號 ， 有助緩解股 、
債市場壓力。
◼ 美伊衝突再升級：美國近期擴大對伊朗相關軍事目標的打擊行動，伊朗亦採取報復措施，市場

擔憂中東原油供應及荷姆茲海峽運輸安全受到干擾 ， 布蘭特原油價格已升至每桶95 美元附近 ，
進一步推升全球通膨預期，增加市場對未來貨幣政策路徑的不確定性。
◼ AI 行情分化，Nvidia 收購開源 AI 開發平台 Hugging Face：Snowflake 公布優於預期的財
報後股價大漲，並帶動 ServiceNow、Salesforce 及 Adobe 等軟體股走強，反觀博通雖然財
報優於預期，但第四季展望及對客製AI 晶片競爭疑慮壓抑股價表現。Nvidia 宣布以約 129 億
美元收購全球最大的開源 AI 開發平台 Hugging Face，意味 Nvidia 正由 GPU 硬體進一步延
伸至模型、資料集及 AI 開發者生態系，藉此鞏固其在全球 AI 基礎建設領域的核心地位。
市場展望與操作策略
◼ AI 題材擴散，科技股中期向上趨勢料不變：近期股市修正主要來自美債殖利率攀升，壓縮部
分科技股的評價空間，半導體族群因而承壓。然而，AI 變現題材接棒，資金由晶片端輪動至軟
體應用層，預料 AI 應用商機持續發酵。惟建議適度降低高評價、高擁擠度及高度依賴遠期獲

利族群的配置比重 。 此外 ， 投資人可透過定時定額或利用市場拉回時分批布局具獲利成長潛力
的台股及日股。
◼ 3~10 年美國公債/投資級債為債市首選：美國通膨及需求數據仍展現韌性，使市場對年底前
升息的預期再度升溫，對美國財政赤字、公債供給及長期通膨風險仍要求較高的風險溢酬。考
量目前 3 至 10 年期美國公債及投資級債可鎖定較高收益率，且受長天期財政風險影響相對較
小，因此現階段 3 至 10 年期美國公債及投資級債的風險報酬結構仍相對具吸引力。

Document 033: 2026年8月宏观数据点评：生产提速，需求仍降

### Page 1

生产提速，需求仍降
——2026 年 8 月宏观数据点评
[Table_Industry]日期: shzqdatemark [Table_Summary] 主要观点
生产强于需求
作为经济成效综合体现的工业生产增速有所提速。三大门类产业增速
除公共事业外均有所回升。8 月主要行业中纺织、医药、通用设备、
黑色金属冶炼有所回升，其中医药回升较多，汽车生产持平上月。
固定投资增速开年实现逆转，由负转正，一季度维持稳定运行，但二
季度以来持续负增。分项来看，制造业投资下降 2.3%，基础设施投资
同比下降 4.0%，跌幅继续扩大。目前，中央预算内投资已经基本下达
完毕，地方政府专项债券的发行使用进度加快，新型政策性金融工具
资金也将抓紧投放，“六张网”建设有序推进。政策支持下，有望激
发投资活力。全国房地产开发投资降幅再度扩大。房地产投资继续拖
累固定投资整体增速，地产投资仍待企稳。
8 月份，社会消费品零售总额 39824 亿元，同比增长 0.4%，小幅回

落。具体来看，各品类消费除金银珠宝、汽车、中西药品外均有所回
升，通讯器材消费回升显著，金银珠宝跌幅扩大，汽车消费仍偏低
迷。8 月商品零售有所回落、餐饮消费增速有所下滑。
加力扩大内需，优化供给
二季度以来，需求放缓，投资持续负增，二季度经济增速较一季度有
所回落。但上半年 4.7%的经济增速也为完成全年目标打下了较好的基
础。8 月生产表现继续强于需求。政策方面，政治局会议提出实施好
更加积极的财政政策和适度宽松的货币政策，充分发挥各项存量政策
效能，及时谋划出台务实管用的增量政策，加大逆周期调节力度。针
对需求方面，会议指出要有效扩大国内需求，适应不同群体消费需求
扩大优质供给，挖掘服务消费潜力；扎实推进“六张网”规划建设。
我们认为在政策助力下，加力扩大内需、优化供给，推动经济持续向
新向优向好发展，努力实现“十五五”良好开局。
 风险提示
地缘政治事件恶化，国际金融形势改变，中美政策超预期变化。

1 事件：8 月份宏观数据公布
工业生产加快。8 月份，规模以上工业增加值同比实际增长
5.2%（增加值增速均为扣除价格因素的实际增长率），增速比上
月加快 0.7 个百分点。从环比看，8 月份，规模以上工业增加值比
上月增长 0.54%。1—8 月份，规模以上工业增加值同比增长 5.3%。
固定资产跌幅扩大。1—8 月份，全国固定资产投资（不含农
户）293092 亿元，同比下降 7.2%。其中，知识产权产品投资同比
增长 9.2%。
消费小幅回落。1—8 月份，社会消费品零售总额 327569 亿元，
同比增长 1.1%。其中，除汽车以外的消费品零售额 301764 亿元，
增长 2.7%。8 月份，社会消费品零售总额 39824 亿元，同比增长
0.4%。其中，除汽车以外的消费品零售额 36545 亿元，增长 2.5%。
表 1：本期经济数据增速（%）
本期值 前值
工业增加值（当月） 5.2 4.5

社会消费品零售（当月） 0.4 0.6
资料来源：统计局，IFIND，上海证券研究所
2 事件解析：数据特征和变动原因
2.1 工业生产有所提速
8 月工业生产有所加快，同比增速回升 0.7 个百分点。分三大
门类看，8 月份，采矿业增加值同比下降 1.4%，制造业增长 6.1%，
电力、热力、燃气及水生产和供应业增长 4.9%。三大门类产业增
速除公共事业外均有所回升。从产量的增速来看，原煤有所回升，
原油产量持平上月；制造业方面，除工业机器人、乙烯外均有所
回落；公共事业方面，发电量有所下降、天然气产量有所回升。
另外出口交货值有所回升。

比下降 7.2%，跌幅进一步加深。其中，第一产业投资 5832 亿元，
同比下降 2.4%；第二产业投资 113094 亿元，下降 2.9%；第三产

固定投资增速开年实现逆转，由负转正，一季度维持稳定运
行，但二季度以来持续负增。分项来看，制造业投资下降 2.3%，
基础设施投资同比下降 4.0%，跌幅继续扩大。目前，中央预算内
投资已经基本下达完毕，地方政府专项债券的发行使用进度加快，
新型政策性金融工具资金也将抓紧投放，“六张网”建设有序推
进。政策支持下，有望激发投资活力。
图 3：固定投资完成额增速（累计同比，%）
资料来源：IFIND，上海证券研究所
2.4 地产投资仍是拖累
1 — 8 月 份 ， 全 国 房 地 产 开 发 投 资 47979 亿 元 ， 同 比 下 降
19.9%，降幅仍在扩大。分项来看，地产销售面积跌幅扩大；供给
端新开工、施工面积跌幅扩大。从房企开发资金的来源来看，房

地产资金跌幅有所扩大，但分项中仅个人按揭项跌幅仍在改善。
地产投资仍待企稳。

费品零售额增长 1.6%。城镇消费继续回落，乡村消费维持稳定。

3 事件影响：对经济和市场
3.1 生产强于需求
作为经济成效综合体现的工业生产增速有所提速。三大门类
产业增速除公共事业外均有所回升。8 月主要行业中纺织、医药、
通用设备、黑色金属冶炼有所回升，其中医药回升较多，汽车生
产持平上月。
固定投资增速开年实现逆转，由负转正，一季度维持稳定运
行，但二季度以来持续负增。分项来看，制造业投资下降 2.3%，
基础设施投资同比下降 4.0%，跌幅继续扩大。目前，中央预算内
投资已经基本下达完毕，地方政府专项债券的发行使用进度加快，
新型政策性金融工具资金也将抓紧投放，“六张网”建设有序推
进。政策支持下，有望激发投资活力。全国房地产开发投资降幅
再度扩大。房地产投资继续拖累固定投资整体增速，地产投资仍
待企稳。
8 月份，社会消费品零售总额 39824 亿元，同比增长 0.4%，

小幅回落。具体来看，各品类消费除金银珠宝、汽车、中西药品
外均有所回升，通讯器材消费回升显著，金银珠宝跌幅扩大，汽
车消费仍偏低迷。8 月商品零售有所回落、餐饮消费增速有所下滑。

4 事件预测：趋势判断
4.1 加力扩大内需，优化供给
二季度以来，需求放缓，投资持续负增，二季度经济增速较
一季度有所回落。但上半年 4.7%的经济增速也为完成全年目标打
下了较好的基础。8 月生产表现继续强于需求。政策方面，政治局
会议提出实施好更加积极的财政政策和适度宽松的货币政策，充
分发挥各项存量政策效能，及时谋划出台务实管用的增量政策，
加大逆周期调节力度。针对需求方面，会议指出要有效扩大国内
需求，适应不同群体消费需求扩大优质供给，挖掘服务消费潜力；
扎实推进“六张网”规划建设。我们认为在政策助力下，加力扩
大内需、优化供给，推动经济持续向新向优向好发展，努力实现
“十五五”良好开局。
5 风险提示：
地缘政治事件恶化，国际金融形势改变，中美政策超预期变
化。

朋程 (8255 TT)  Neutral

全球車市展望平淡，營運成長溫和，投資評等為 Neutral 
投資建議 ：基於： (1)2Q26 營收成長不如預期，主因歐洲客戶拉
貨放緩。 (2)2H26 因原物料及晶圓代工成本上揚，使獲利持續壓
抑。 (3)  全球車市展望復甦緩慢，加上中國車市內捲嚴重，不利
於朋程評價提升，投資評等為 Neutral 。  
歐洲客戶拉貨放緩， 2Q26 本業獲利 YOY 下滑 ：朋程 2Q26 營
收 20.32 億元 (QOQ-0.3%/YOY-6.1%) ，不如市場預期，主因受歐
洲客戶拉貨放緩，使 48V MOSFET 模組出貨量顯著放緩，毛利
率 26.98% ，與前季維持相當；費用率 20.60% ，與前季一致，本
業獲利 1.3 億元， YOY 下滑 44.5% ，惟業外方面，因 25 年受匯
損低基期影響，稅後淨利為 1.46 億元 (QOQ-
15.4%/YOY+276.3%) ，稅後 EPS 1.44 元。  
預估 2H26 營運逐季增溫，惟原物料及代工成本上揚，壓抑獲
利：展望 2H26 ，受惠超效能二極體 (ULLD) 訂單需求暢旺，加上
高毛利客製化 IGBT 之工業產品於 4Q26 開始小量量產，預估朋
程 2H26 營運有望呈現溫和增溫的態勢， 3Q26 及 4Q26 營收分
別有望季增 3.7% 及 5.1%，然而毛利率方面，因受原物料銅價上
揚及上游晶圓代工漲價影響，使 2H26 毛利率有所壓抑，研究部
預估較 1H26 平均毛利率約衰退 1.2pt 。預估朋程 2026 年營收為
83.88 億元 (YOY+3.5%) ，毛 利 率26.35%(YOY-0.4%pt) ，稅 後EPS 
6.06 元 (YOY-4%) ，獲利較 2025 年呈微幅衰退。  
展望 2027 年， ULLD 動能續強，靜待工業新品實質貢獻： 展望
2027 年，因應歐美碳排法規趨嚴，車廠追求高 CP 值解決方案，
進而推升 ULLD 相關需求，抵銷傳統二極體及 LLD 需求下滑影
響。而 SiC 方面，項目尚在送樣驗證階段，靜待實質貢獻發酵。
然毛利率則預期因晶圓代工及原物料成本持續上揚，將抵銷產
品組合轉佳影響，預估朋程 2027 年毛利率將呈持穩態勢。綜上
所述 ， 研究部預估朋程2027 年營收為 93.06 億元 (YOY+10.9%) ，
毛利率 26.53% ，稅後 EPS 7.29 元 (YOY+20.3%) 。

2 群益投顧 
  ESG(Environment, Social, Governance) ：  
朋程科技身為全球車用二極體與功率模組龍頭，將 ESG 視為企業永續的核
心，積極推動「綠色製造、誠信治理、社會共好」三大面向。  
一、環境保護（ Environmental ）：  
碳管理與淨零路徑：遵循 ISO 14064-1 完成全廠區溫室氣體盤查，推動 ISO 
50001 能源管理系統，並訂定 2050 淨零排放（ Net Zero ）長期目標。綠色產
品與節能低碳：主力產品「超高效率二極體（ LL-DIODE ）」顯著提升發電
機效率，協助全球燃油車及混動車降低二氧化碳排放。積極佈局 SiC（碳化
矽）與 IGBT 功率模組，推動電動車（ EV）與綠能逆變器高能效轉型。綠色
製造與資源循環：導入廢棄物資源化與節水設備，達成 ISO 14001 與 ISO 
45001 認證，落實有害物質禁用 (RoHS/REACH 規範 )。  
二、社會責任（ Social ）：  
人本關懷與職業安全：打造多元平等職場（ DEI ），提供完善薪酬福利與育
兒支持；連續多年獲得職業安全衛生優良單位人才培育與技術傳承：建立產
學合作計畫，補助員工持續教育，重視車用半導體研發人才之長期留任。  
供應鏈永續管理：制定《供應商行為準則》，要求供應商簽署不使用衝突礦
石（ Conflict-Free Minerals ）聲明，落實供應鏈環境與人權評核。社會參與
與公益：長期投入偏鄉教育、在地社區關懷與綠色植樹活動，回饋營運據點
周邊社區。  
三、公司治理（ Governance ）：  
誠信經營與資訊透明：設有獨立董事與審計委員會，定期進行板塊運作與董
事會績效評估 ， 保障股東與利益關係人權益 。 資訊安全與永續報告 ： 取得ISO 
27001 資訊安全管理認證，定期發布符合 GRI 準則與 SASB 規範之 ESG 永
續報告書。風險控管與韌性：建立地緣政治、氣候變遷風險（ TCFD ）與供
應鏈中斷之營運持續計畫（ BCP ）。

### Page 3

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 9 月 10 日

3 群益投顧 
公司簡介：  
朋程 (8255 TT) 成立於 1998 年，總部位於桃園市蘆竹區，是一家車用二極體
與功率半導體廠，為全球汽車發電機整流二極體最大供應商。公司目前業務
與產品分為四大核心領域， 1.一般整流二極體 (Standard Diode) ：主要應用於
傳統燃油車發電機，提供基本整流與車用電源轉化功能； 2.高效能二极體
(LLD / ULLD) ：包含高效能與超高效能二極體，具備低功耗與高轉換效率特
性，以因應各國車輛碳排放與節能法規要求； 3.新能源車功率模組 (EV Power 
Module) ：專注於油電混合動力車之逆變器、主驅動模組，並透過旗下子公
司安捷特布局矽 (Si) 與第三代半導體碳化矽 (SiC) 、氮化鎵 (GaN) 晶片設計與
封裝； 4.工控與特殊應用 (Industrial & Others) ：包含工業用 IGBT 模組、 AI
伺服器電源管理及儲能系統功率半導體解決方案。 2026 年銷售區域以亞洲
市場 (含中國大陸 )為主要營收來源，北美與歐洲次之。 2025 年營收產品
別 :Diode 佔 29% 、 LLD 佔 28% 、 ULLD 佔 21% 、 xEV 佔 17% 。

歐洲客戶拉貨放緩， 2Q26 本業獲利 YOY 下滑：  
朋程 2Q26 營收 20.32 億元 (QOQ-0.3%/YOY-6.1%) ，不如市場預期，主因受
歐洲客戶拉貨放緩，使 48V MOSFET 模組出貨量顯著放緩，毛利率 26.98% ，
與前季維持相當，費用率 20.60% ，與前季一致，本業獲利 1.3 億元， YOY
下滑 44.5% ，惟業外方面，因 25 年受匯損低基期影響，稅後淨利為 1.46 億
元 (QOQ-15.4%/YOY+276.3%) ，稅後 EPS 1.44 元。

預估 2H26 營運逐季增溫，惟原物料及代工成本上揚，壓抑獲利：  
展望 2H26 ，受惠超效能二極體 (ULLD) 訂單需求暢旺，加上高毛利客製化
IGBT 之工業產品於 4Q26 開始小量量產，預估朋程 2H26 營運有望呈現溫
和增溫的態勢， 3Q26 及 4Q26 營收分別有望季增 3.7% 及 5.1% ，然而毛利率
方面，因受原物料銅價上揚及上游晶圓代工漲價影響，使 2H26 毛利率有所
壓抑，研究部預估較 1H26 平均毛利率約衰退 1.2pt 。整體產能預估方面，
Diode( 一般二極體 )約 1.3 億 ~1.4 億顆， LLD( 低功耗二極體 )7200 萬 ~7500 萬
顆， ULLD( 超高效二極體 )4000 萬 ~4200 萬顆， xEV( 新能源車模組 )接近 300
萬顆。綜上所述，預估朋程 2026 年營收為 83.88 億元 (YOY+3.5%) ，毛利率
26.35%(YOY-0.4%pt) ，稅後淨利為 6.17 億元 (YOY-4%) ，稅後 EPS 6.06 元，
獲利較 2025 年呈微幅衰退。

展望 2027 年， ULLD 接單動能續強，靜待工業事業發酵：  
展望 2027 年，因各國碳排法規越來越嚴 格，車廠需要降低燃油車的油耗與
廢氣。 ULLD 可以直接替換傳統整流二極體，提升發電機效率、降低發熱量
與碳排，不需要車廠大幅改動整車電氣架構，為成本最低的減碳方案，使目
前 ULLD 接單動能續強。 ULLD 單價是傳統二極體的達 6 倍，在產線滿載
與出貨增長的帶動下，可有效填補傳統二極體及 LLD 下滑影響。此外，新
產品方面，公司表示， IGBT 新產能將於 4Q26 起開始放量，單月出貨量將
達 150K ，並預計於 1Q27 單季出貨量將從 500K 逐季提升至 800K ，此項產
品高於公司平均毛利率。而 SIC 方面，項目尚在送樣驗證階段，將聚焦 AI、

### Page 4

4 群益投顧 
HVDC 、 PSU 、 BBU 、儲能 ESS 與工控領域，在 HVDC 架構下， SiC 需求從
650V 延伸至 3300V 高壓規格，公司出貨將以 600V~700V 為主，主要應用
包括工控、家用冷氣、空調、半導體設備、無人機等。毛利率展望方面，因
預期晶圓代工成本持續上揚，抵銷產品組合轉佳影響，預估毛利率呈持穩態
勢。綜上所述，研究部預估朋程 2027 年營收為 93.06 億元 (YOY+10.9%) ，
毛利率 26.53% ，稅後 EPS 7.29 元 (YOY+20.3%) 。

  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

Document 207: AI, Ageing and the Budget_ Five Takeaways from the 2026 IGR (2026-09-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI, Ageing and the Budget_ Five Takeaways from the 2026 IGR (2026-09-23).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-09-23
Pages: 15
Status: ok

### Page 1

#businessconditions #confidence
AI, Ageing and the Budget: Five Takeaways from the 2026 IGR
By Pat Bustamante
Senior Economist, Westpac
18:21 September 21 2026 Share
The 2026 Intergenerational Report is the 7th in a series of such reports, dating back to
2002. The IGR sets out a budget scenario highlighting potential budget challenges over
coming decades.

### Page 2

The familiar theme is that Australia’s ageing population will put upward pressure on expenditures and
downward pressure on revenues, creating a budget challenge.
In the 2026 edition, several forces are identified as continuing to shape Australia’s economy over the
coming decades: the artificial intelligence revolution; geopolitical fragmentation; energy transition;
ageing and the care economy; and Australia’s industrial transformation.
Below are the five key themes that stood out in this year's IGR: AI to be a game-changer for

productivity; Australians will live longer; NDIS reforms are crucial to budget sustainability, but knock on
to state budgets; Bracket creep has its limits, and future spending will need to be funded elsewhere;
and Higher costs of capital here to stay.
1. AI to be a game-changer for productivity
The IGR identifies AI as having the potential to be a game-changer for productivity growth. We agree
and have written extensively about the data centre and renewable energy investment surge. This

investment will boost value added and jobs in the near term. However, the real payoff will come when
the technology is widely adopted and businesses redesign processes to get the most out of AI.
The ultimate gains are difficult to estimate, in part because of the speed of development in the
technology. Another reason is that technological change at this scale does not simply augment or
automate existing roles but creates new roles, services and goods. The Productivity Commission

estimates that AI could boost multifactor productivity in Australia by at least 2.3 per cent over the next
decade, but this estimate was published in December, before the step-change in model capability we
saw early this year.
The IGR shows that productivity matters for the budget: productivity growth 0.4ppt higher than the
assumed 1.2% per year would reduce gross debt by 25ppts of GDP in 2065–66. Of course, this
assumes that fiscal policy does not respond to the faster trend growth that implies.

### Page 3

2. Australians will live longer
The IGR projects that life expectancy will continue to increase to 86.1 for males and 89.5 for females
by 2065, around five years longer than today. This presents both opportunities and challenges.
Older Australians are remaining in the workforce for longer, boosting labour supply and economic
activity. At the same time, rising demand for health, aged care and pension spending will place
increasing pressure on government budgets.

This is one area where AI, along with governance changes, could deliver outsized benefits. While an
ageing population will increase demand for government services, productivity-enhancing technologies
can improve service delivery and support prevention.
We know that the non-market sector (including health care, education and public administration) is the
largest it has ever been, with the expansion mainly driven by health services. Notwithstanding

measurement issues, we know that productivity levels in this sector has declined recently. Greater use
of evidence-based practice, preventive care, and AI-enabled technologies in health and aged care
could help improve productivity across this rapidly expanding part of the economy.

### Page 4

Demographic change will increase reliance on a smaller share of the population to fund the health,
aged care and other services required by a growing number of older Australians. We know that the
average fiscal contribution of an individual becomes negative later in life as government spending on
health and aged care rises. By contrast, people in their prime working years make the largest net
contribution to the budget through higher workforce participation and tax payments. 
This reinforces the importance of lifting productivity growth, both to support living standards and to help
meet rising spending pressures without placing an ever-greater burden on the working-age population.

3. NDIS reforms are crucial to budget sustainability, but knock on to state budgets 
The IGR highlights just how significant the NDIS reforms are for the fiscal outlook. Without the reforms,
payments would be around 1 percentage point of GDP higher over the medium term. The savings are
substantial and reinforce the importance of full implementation, a point we have previously made
(2026–27 Australian Federal Budget | Westpac IQ ). 
The report focuses on the Commonwealth budget, but the implications for state budgets could also be
significant. To the extent that some costs are shifted to states, a complete assessment of the reforms
requires a consolidated national fiscal perspective.

### Page 5

4. Bracket creep has its limits, and future spending will need to be funded elsewhere
Personal income tax receipts are projected to rise from 12.3% of GDP in 2025-26 to 14.1% of GDP by
2065-66, even with the Government capping tax as a share of the economy.
This highlights a structural challenge. As Australia ages, an increasing share of government revenue
will come from a relatively smaller working-age population, while the share of indirect taxes continues

to decline.
The IGR argues that relying ever more heavily on labour income taxation is unlikely to be a sustainable
long-run solution. Future governments may therefore need to look elsewhere for revenue growth. This
could include reforms to indirect taxes, particularly as fuel excise revenues come under pressure from
the transition to electric vehicles.
The report reinforces the need to modernise the tax base as activity shifts towards digital services and

intangible consumption. The revenue challenge is material, with the erosion of indirect tax bases
potentially amounting to close to 1ppt of GDP over coming decades, on top of the effective loss of
tobacco excise in recent years.
The terms of trade are assumed to remain broadly unchanged from their 2027–28 level, underpinning

### Page 6

company tax receipts. This bakes in the sharp near-term decline in iron ore prices that has been
assumed in every budget for more than a decade without eventuating. Despite this, the longer-run
trajectory is increasing uncertain as the world transitions towards net zero.
5. Higher costs of capital here to stay
We have long argued that the era of ultra-low interest rates is unlikely to return. The 2026 IGR
reinforces this view. Despite an improved fiscal position and lower projected debt than in the 2023 IGR,

interest payments are projected to be higher because government borrowing costs are assumed to
remain elevated relative to the post-GFC period.
Total interest payments are projected to rise from 0.9% of GDP in 2025–26 to 1.6% of GDP in 2032–33
before easing to 1.2% of GDP by 2065–66. Importantly, the primary cash balance (excluding interest
payments) is projected to remain in surplus until the mid-2050s. In other words, the budget is projected
to be in deficit largely because of the growing cost of servicing debt, rather than an underlying

imbalance between government revenues and expenditures.
The IGR’s sensitivity analysis highlights how important borrowing costs are for the fiscal outlook. If
bond yields average 5.4 per cent, broadly in line with current levels, the underlying cash deficit would
be around 0.5 percentage points of GDP larger and gross debt would be 6.6 percentage points of GDP
higher by 2065-66.

Document 232: Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, August (2026-09-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, August (2026-09-23).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-09-23
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

#businessconditions #confidence #aud #australia
Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, August
By Luka Belobrajdic
Economist, Westpac
14:40 September 08 2026 Share
A deterioration in profitability and trading conditions sees business conditions fall to their
weakest level since the pandemic, while confidence continues to slide. Conditions –5pts to
–1 / Confidence –2pts to –8.

### Page 2

The August NAB Business Survey was in the field from 24 to 31 August, capturing businesses’ reaction
to ongoing global uncertainty, elevated fuel prices and, at least in part, the upside surprise in July’s
inflation figures. The headline business conditions index fell into negative territory for the first time in six
years, declining 5pts to –1 and marking the weakest result since the height of the COVID-19 pandemic
in 2020. Conditions were relatively stable but sluggish over recent months; today’s result, however, has

pulled the index 7pts below its long-run average, highlighting the risk of further slowing in growth near-
term as businesses grapple with higher interest rates and uncertainty stemming from the Middle East
conflict.
Across the major sub-components, profitability was the primary driver of the deterioration in conditions.
The sub-index fell 10pts to –9, its lowest level since the pandemic and the sharpest decline since
January 2022. At 14pts below its long-run average, the result suggests a growing share of firms are

reporting margin squeeze, likely reflecting the combined impact of elevated costs and higher interest
rates. Trading conditions also weakened materially, falling 5pts to 3 after holding at 8 over the previous
four months. The sub-index now sits 9pts below its long-run average and, like profitability, is at its
weakest level since the pandemic. The result points to a softening in demand conditions despite the
resilience evident in July’s household spending indicator. Employment was broadly unchanged at 3 on

a rounded basis, though eased 1pt in unrounded terms.

### Page 3

Business confidence declined for a second consecutive month, falling 2pts to –8 and extending the
reversal of the recovery that emerged from the conflict lows in March and April. While confidence
remains well above the lows recorded earlier in the year, the August reading remains deeply
pessimistic, sitting 13pts below its long-run average. The deterioration coincides with lingering
uncertainty and persistent cost pressures, with the national average retail petrol price once again lifting

above $2/L in August following the roll-off of the fuel excise discount. The upside surprise in July’s
inflation figures, which prompted markets to increase expectations of further RBA tightening, also likely
weighed on confidence.
Cost growth softened a touch in August. Final product prices declined 0.2ppt to 0.8%qtr, though the
measure remains 0.2ppt above its long-run average. Given the sharp decline in profitability and
continued strength in purchase cost growth, it would not be surprising to see firms seek to rebuild

margins through stronger final product price growth in coming months. Labour cost growth also eased
slightly, down 0.1ppt to 1.9%qtr, though this remains 0.6ppt above its long-run average. Purchase cost
growth, by contrast, rose 0.1ppt to 2.3%qtr and remains elevated at 1.1ppt above its long-run average.
Capacity utilisation retraced around half of July’s gain, declining 0.6ppt to 82.5%. While still 1.1ppt
above its long-run average and continuing to trend higher on a three-month average basis, utilisation

remains below levels observed earlier in the year. Capital expenditure was broadly unchanged in
unrounded terms at 2. Nevertheless, with the sub-index sitting 4pts below its long-run average and

### Page 4

Related articles
continuing to trend lower on a three-month average basis, the result suggests that weakness in
business investment observed in the recent weeks National Accounts may be extending into the
September quarter.

Document 284: FX Daily_ Dollar resilience builds

Source PDF: all report pdf/FX Daily_ Dollar resilience builds.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

Article | 23 September 2026 1
THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article  |  23 September 2026   FX
FX Daily: Dollar resilience builds
The dollar has remained firm despite oil and global risk sentiment
arguing for a correction. Hawkish Fedspeak remains key, and this week
underscores the risk that falling oil prices may offer little support to
EUR/USD, as markets may be more willing to stick with Fed hike
expectations than with the ECB s in an oil price decline. USD upside risks

persist
Markets found little direction in Trump's UN address on Tuesday
USD: Staying strong
The dollar continues to show very good resilience to lower energy prices and a risk-friendly
environment. It’s another sign that the Fed story is dominant, and the hawkish Fedspeak is
enough to keep USD in demand. Richmond Fed President Thomas Barkin, who becomes a voter
in 2027, reinforced that message yesterday, arguing that a single rate hike may not be

enough to bring inflation under control. He also noted that resilient labour market conditions
should keep consumer spending supported, implying that a dovish shift among the hawks may
require clearer signs of labour market softening.
But high-frequency jobs indicators have remained strong. Initial jobless claims are back below
200k and ADP reported a bump in weekly hiring to 20k at the start of September. Consensus is
starting to build around a strong September payroll print – around 80-100k.

### Page 2

THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article | 23 September 2026 2
Geopolitics had a limited impact on FX yesterday. President Trump's UN speech generated few
clear market signals, while constructive talks with Iranian delegates and the reopening of the
Saudi East-West pipeline pushed Brent below $100/bbl. Whether this evolves into a more
sustained decline remains to be seen. From a rates perspective, oil floating around the
$90-100/bbl range is unlikely to force a dovish rethink in market expectations.

We’ll be awaiting more headlines from New York ahead of tomorrow’s Trump-Xi meeting. The
Fedspeak agenda is quiet today, and the data calendar only includes S&P Global PMIs, which
matter less for markets than the ISM surveys. We retain the view that the dollar faces upside
risks over the next couple of weeks, when a revamp of data releases can prompt markets to
add bets on an October hike. In that context, DXY reaching 101.0 remains a very achievable

near-term target.
Francesco Pesole
EUR: Still downside risks
EUR/USD is starting to look a tad cheap, as global equities support the pair’s short-term fair
value. Still, we see little technical justification for fading the current decline before 1.140-1.142,
where EUR/USD would be breaching a 1.5 standard deviation misvaluation band, assuming
rate differentials remain unchanged.
This week underscored another risk: when oil prices fall, markets may price out ECB tightening

faster than Fed tightening. That was a major drag on EUR/USD in June, and any hint of a
dovish rethink from ECB speakers could trigger a leg lower.
The next few weeks will determine whether either the ECB or the Fed goes for an October hike.
While we still expect the next move from both central banks to come in December, any earlier
tightening appears more likely to come from the Fed than the ECB. That leaves the balance of
risks skewed to the downside for EUR/USD.

Eyes will be on PMIs today, which in the eurozone have greater monetary policy implications
than elsewhere. Expectations are for a slight softening in the eurozone-wide composite gauge
from 52.0 to 51.7. Let’s see whether budget concerns in France and political developments in
Germany have had any impact on sentiment. Meanwhile, the ECB speakers' calendar remains
busy today.
Francesco Pesole
HUF: A well-paused decision
The National Bank of Hungary meeting delivered exactly what the market expected – a pause

in the rate-cutting cycle and a reduction of the inflation target from 3.0% to 2.5%. From the
market's perspective, this was a case of ticking boxes that had been anticipated since the April

### Page 3

THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article | 23 September 2026 3
general election. The market remained virtually unchanged in front-end rates following the
NBH press conference, with pricing in less than one rate cut over the next 6-9 months,
assuming a positive BUBOR spread over the NBH key rate. The curve has flattened significantly
over the past two weeks, aligning with our earlier expectations outlined in our NBH preview.
Nevertheless, we see scope for further flattening, even though the curve is already deeply

inverted. At the same time, the front end offers a buffer against a potential geopolitical
escalation or a spike in energy prices. We believe the market would not hesitate to price in rate
hikes at the front end of the curve if energy prices rise again and Hungarian inflation climbs in
the coming months (NBH forecast of 3.1% average inflation for next year). Meanwhile, the long
end still offers room for convergence trades, and long-term yields should grind lower.

The combination of a lowered inflation target and a halt to rate cuts creates a very positive
environment for the FX market. On the other hand, the global backdrop remains largely
negative, despite the recent relief rally. The US dollar is at record strength, EUR/HUF remains
highly sensitive to energy prices, and Fed rate hikes could trigger renewed pressure on
emerging market currencies, which have shown remarkable resilience so far. The overall

picture is thus very mixed, and global factors could easily take the driver's seat. Although our
bias remains bullish for the forint, and we could retest the 360 EUR/HUF level, further
developments are heavily dependent on global factors at this moment.
Frantisek Taborsky
CEE: FX stability masks diverging rate outlooks
The region is benefiting from improved global sentiment and lower energy prices, prompting
markets to scale back expectations for aggressive rate hikes. Czech rates, the most hawkish in

the region, price around 100bp of tightening, versus 85bp in Poland. Today’s calendar is again
light, with only August Polish labour market data due. Despite continued rates volatility, FX
remains stable. In our view, opposing forces are broadly balancing out and keeping CEE
currencies steady: a stronger US dollar is weighing on the region, while improved sentiment
and lower energy prices have yet to offset its impact.
Local drivers remain limited this week, and fresh catalysts may take time to emerge. We

expect a more hawkish CNB to push EUR/CZK lower again, but this is unlikely in the near term
unless Friday’s minutes strike a surprisingly hawkish tone. By contrast, we see further upside in
EUR/PLN given the central bank’s dovish stance. Potential government measures to cut petrol
prices could complicate the outlook: if approved, they might allow the central bank to
postpone rate hikes, while also weakening the interest-rate differential and putting downward

pressure on the zloty.
Frantisek Taborsky

◆ The economy remains on the recovery track despite weakness in some
areas
Annualized real GDP growth in the second quarter was +1.1% QoQ, with growth
staying in positive territory for three straight quarters. Although personal
consumption exhibited weakness and capital investment declined, overall growth
was lifted by foreign demand. Exports, particularly of services, increased, and the
drop in imports of Middle Eastern crude oil also helped foreign demand turn positive.

◆ Housing starts are weak
Housing starts surged 18.6% YoY in June. They have continued to rise on a YoY
basis, but the jump was mainly due to a rebound from the slump in the same month
of the previous year. The recovery in the number of starts is actually moderate,
reflecting higher interest rates and soaring construction costs.
◆ Earthquake impact will need to be watched
Damage from the earthquake that occurred in Kumamoto Prefecture at the end of

July is weighing on economic activity in the affected areas. Although moves toward
recovery can be seen, with many companies having already resumed operations,
the impact could become prolonged in the following scenarios:
The first is if damage to capital stock such as factories and roads has been severe.
Some companies have sustained damage to production facilities, making an early
resumption of operations difficult in some cases.
The second is if a downturn in tourism demand occurs. According to some media

reports, there has been an uptick in cancellations of accommodation reservations in
Kumamoto Prefecture and neighboring prefectures since the earthquake. After the
2016 Kumamoto earthquake, the decline in travel demand from Japanese visitors
was short lived, but lower demand from foreign tourists persisted for a long period.
Annualized real GDP growth was +1.1% quarter over quarter (QoQ) in Q2 2026

Exports and inbound demand remain solid despite weakness in some areas
◆ Goods exports are on the verge of picking up
Goods exports are treading water. A sharp rise in memory chip prices is among
factors pushing up export prices, resulting in record high levels of exports in value
terms recently. On the other hand, the increase in volume terms is limited. One

possible reason is that Japan's export competitiveness has declined in fields such as
semiconductors, where global demand is growing.
Looking ahead, exports are expected to pick up. Capital investment by chipmakers
is expanding worldwide, and this should lift exports of semiconductor manufacturing
equipment, an area in which Japan has strength. However, continued caution is
needed regarding the effects of prolonged turmoil in the Middle East. A global

slowdown due to elevated crude oil prices could weigh on exports.
◆ Reluctance to visit among Chinese travelers is weighing on demand for
travel to Japan
Inbound demand is currently soft. The Chinese government's advisory urging its
citizens to refrain from traveling to Japan is having an adverse impact. The number
of Chinese visitors to Japan has fallen to about half the level prior to the advisory.
Meanwhile, the number of tourists from countries other than China remains firm.

Inbound demand is expected to stay robust going forward. Continued increases in
tourists from countries other than China will remain a supporting factor. The
expectation that the yen will remain weak will also act to push up inbound
consumption. However, there are two downside risks going forward:
The first is that the Chinese government keeps the advisory in place for a
prolonged period. When China banned group travel to South Korea in 2017, tourist

numbers stayed low for two to three years.
The second is the impact of higher crude oil prices. Major airlines have embarked
on phased increases in fuel surcharges, which could weigh on inbound demand
during the summer.

### Page 6

Personal consumption is gradually recovering thanks to a favorable income environment
◆ Higher disposable incomes are supporting personal consumption
Personal consumption continues to gradually recover. While consumption of
non-durable goods remains weak, services consumption is firm, particularly in
amusement-related services. In addition, consumption of durable goods has
increased significantly. Temporary factors such as the abolition of the environmental

performance tax on automobiles (since April 2026) and the tightening of
energy-efficiency standards for air conditioners (effective April 2027) have also
contributed to this rise.
A favorable income environment can also be pointed to as a background factor
behind the recovery in personal consumption. YoY real disposable income growth has
generally been positive since mid-2024, and the pace of increase has recently
accelerated. Employee remuneration is rising, partly as a result of agreements made

during the shunto (spring wage negotiations), and net asset income is also increasing
at a faster clip as companies hike dividends in line with stock price rises.
◆ Stock price developments could be a source of concern for personal
consumption
Personal consumption is expected to continue its gradual recovery going forward. In
addition to the favorable income environment and government policy measures to
curb price increases, a recovery in consumer confidence driven by easing tensions in

the Middle East will also provide a tailwind. Recently, the Consumer Confidence Index
has been showing signs of bottoming out.
However, a reverse wealth effect stemming from falling stock prices is a potential
cause for concern. The Nikkei Stock Average, which reached a new high in June, has
been struggling to push higher since the start of July. Estimates suggest the wealth
effect is concentrated among elderly households, but in recent years working-age

households have also increased their holdings of equity-related assets, and the
positive wealth effect for them may have supported consumption to some extent. For
this reason, if stock prices were to fall significantly, there is a danger of downward
pressure on consumption across a wide range of households.

セン
To
◆ The unemployment rate has settled in the mid-2% range
While the employment situation is generally favorable, weakness can be seen in
some areas. The unemployment rate has remained in the mid-2% range since 2022.
The number of employed persons in non-manufacturing, where business conditions
are favorable, is holding firm, while in manufacturing the number remains at a level
below that seen before the COVID-19 pandemic.
In the background to the drop in manufacturing employment is a slump in

production activity. Manufacturing output has been shrinking due to the effects of
U.S. tariff policy, soaring raw material prices, and a decline in the international
competitiveness of Japanese firms. As a result, pressure to reduce headcounts has
been building. Even in non-manufacturing, some industries closely linked to
manufacturing, such as transportation and wholesale/retail, are also exhibiting
weakness in labor demand.
◆ The employment situation could remain fragile in the near term

Looking ahead at the employment situation in the short term, a sense of
stagnation may persist. The contraction in output in the manufacturing sector will
continue to weigh on labor demand. Although production activity has been brisker
recently, particularly in semiconductor-related industries, the employment-creation
capacity of such industries is small. The employment-inducement coefficient per unit
of new demand tends to be lower than in other sectors, meaning that the industries

lack the strength to serve as drivers of employment.
However, in the medium to long term, the unemployment rate is expected to see a
gradual decline. Demand for labor is projected to stay solid, especially in
face-to-face services such as medical/nursing care and accommodation and
eating/drinking services, but the number of people seeking employment from the
non-labor force population has declined significantly due to the expansion in elderly
and female workforce participation. With limited room for expansion in the labor

supply, the job market is expected to tighten further.

The government is curbing inflation
◆ Policy factors are pushing down the inflation rate
In June, core inflation (all items, less fresh food) was 1.6%, staying under 2%
for the fifth consecutive month. Food price inflation has slowed, and a major

reason for this is a fallback in rice prices, which soared last year. Gasoline
subsidies are also continuing to curtail energy price increases.
Inflation looks set to hover in the 2% range in the second half of 2026. Although
energy subsidies and other government measures will soften inflationary pressure
to some extent, with imported inflationary pressure building, prices are expected
to be pushed up as higher costs are passed through to selling prices. From

FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items will
serve to keep prices down, and inflation is expected to drop into the 1% range.
◆ The Bank of Japan (BOJ) will keep its policy rate unchanged
At its July monetary policy meeting, the BOJ decided to keep its policy rate
unchanged.
Long-term interest rates remain high. This reflects concerns about inflation
resulting from the increase in oil prices and expectations of an expansion in fiscal

expenditure.
The BOJ is expected to step up the pace of interest rate hikes while closely
monitoring the impact of higher rates on the real economy and financial/capital
markets. The next rate hike is expected to be in September.
Long-term interest rates look likely to rise gradually, reflecting heightened
anticipation of rate hikes by the BOJ and increased fiscal spending by the
government. If jitters about a deterioration in fiscal discipline resulting from such

measures as energy subsidies or the consumption tax cut take hold, there is also
a risk that long-term interest rates will rise.

◆ Surging crude oil prices have become a source of upward pressure on
prices
With crude oil prices having spiked against the backdrop of the worsening situation
in the Middle East, corporate input costs have risen sharply, and pressure for price
pass-through is intensifying. Looking at the Final Demand-Intermediate Demand
price indexes, the margin of price increases at the upstream stage far exceeds that at
the downstream stage, and price pass-through pressure is also substantial at the

downstream stage.
According to estimates based on past data, the impact of rising oil prices involves
a time lag. Specifically, the push-up effect on the consumer price inflation rate
increases for around 10 months and continues to exert influence for a long period
thereafter.
◆ Price pass-through willingness is also stronger than before
Notably, food product manufacturers are planning to raise prices for a huge
number of items during the summer and beyond. According to Teikoku Databank, the

number of items slated for price increases as of the end of July, when it conducted its
latest survey, was higher than in the May and June surveys.
If government subsidies for electricity/gas bills and gasoline prices are scaled back
or ended, both energy and food prices will increase concurrently, and the consumer
price inflation rate may rise again.
Attention also needs to be paid to the possibility that price pass-through driven by
elevated oil prices will become prolonged. In the 2020s, the rate of price increases in

transactions between companies has been higher than in the 2010s, and firms'
medium-term outlooks for selling prices have also shifted upward. Events that trigger
cost increases, such as heightened geopolitical tensions, have been occurring
repeatedly, and companies may be more willing to pass through higher costs to
prices than they were before.

### Page 10

The economy is expected to continue to recover, but downside risks are present
◆ The economy is projected to stay on the recovery track
The Japanese economy should continue its gradual recovery. WTI crude oil
futures prices are expected to fall moderately going forward.
In the corporate sector, government support for investment is providing a tailwind,
and demand for investment for labor saving and digital transformation remains
robust. In addition, rising global demand for AI-related goods should support exports.

Supply chain disruptions resulting from the deterioration in the Middle East situation
will begin to ease, and corporate production activity is expected to gradually recover.
In the household sector, inflationary pressure is expected to strengthen from the
summer onward due to higher crude oil prices. In response, the government will
probably ease the inflationary pressure through measures such as energy subsidies.
While the pace of increase in personal consumption is likely to slow somewhat,

consumption is expected to remain resilient amid continued high wage growth. From
FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items should also
support personal consumption.
◆ The Middle East situation and rising interest rates constitute risks
Real GDP growth is projected to remain solid, registering +0.8% in FY2026 and
+0.9% in FY2027.
However, if the situation in the Middle East worsens again or becomes prolonged,
there is a risk that downward pressure on the economy will intensify, mainly through

three channels: 1) weaker exports, 2) raw material supply constraints, and 3) rising
prices. In addition, if market concerns about the fiscal stance of the Takaichi
government grow, the yen could weaken further, and long-term interest rates could
rise, potentially forcing the scaling-back or scrapping of expansionary policies such
as investment support measures, energy subsidies, and lower consumption tax.
Monthly Report of Prospects for Japan's Economy August 2026

Document 345: RBNZは緩やかな引き締め示唆､NZドル相場の行方はどうなる？ ～２会合連続の利上げにより､先行きは従来想定より利上げペースの後退を示唆～

Source PDF: all report pdf/RBNZは緩やかな引き締め示唆､NZドル相場の行方はどうなる？ ～２会合連続の利上げにより､先行きは従来想定より利上げペースの後退を示唆～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-09-02
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年 9 月 2 日

1/4 
RBNZ は緩やかな引き締め示唆､NZ ドル相場の行方はどうなる？ 
～２会合連続の利上げにより､先行きは従来想定より利上げペースの後退を示唆～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ RBNZ（ニュージーランド準備銀行）は９月１～２日の定例理事会において、政策金利（ＯＣ
Ｒ）を 25bp 引き上げて 2.75％とすることを決定し、７月に続く２会合連続の利上げとなっ
た。 背景には、 イラン情勢の悪化による原油高を受けてインフレが加速し、 ４-６月のインフ
レ率が前年比＋4.1％と RBNZ の目標レンジ（２～３％）を上回ったことがある。一方、コア
インフレ率は同＋2.5％と目標内にとどまり、 雇用不安や賃金上昇圧力の弱さもあって、 エネ
ルギー以外の物価上昇は比較的落ち着いている。 
○ RBNZ は、燃料価格上昇によるインフレ加速は認めつつ、インフレ率は 2027 年半ばに目標レ
ンジへ回帰し、その後２％に収束するとの見通しを示した。景気については、輸出や輸出価
格の上昇が所得・投資を押し上げる一方、雇用不安や住宅価格低迷が家計消費や住宅投資の
重しになっているとした。ただし、先行きは輸出の堅調さや家計消費の拡大により景気回復
が広がるとした。追加利上げの可能性は残すものの、今回の利上げによって将来の大幅利上
げの必要性は低下したとの認識を示しており、ブレマン総裁も今後はこれまでの利上げ効果
や景気回復の広がりを見極める姿勢を強調した。このため、先行きの利上げペースは従来よ
り緩やかになる可能性がある。11 月の総選挙を前に、 利上げによる住宅ローン負担増を巡る
政治的な議論も活発化するが、RBNZ は政策判断が選挙に左右されることを否定した。 
○ 金融市場では、米ドル安を追い風に NZ ドルが一時３ヵ月ぶりの高値を付けるなど強含んだ
ものの、 足元ではＦＲＢの利上げ観測による米ドルの持ち直しに加え、RBNZ の今後の利上げ
ペース鈍化が意識され、上値が重くなっている。対円でもこれまでの円安を背景に堅調に推
移してきたが、先行きはＮＺドルへの調整圧力が強まる可能性に注意が必要である。

【イラン情勢の悪化によるインフレ加速を受け､RBNZは２会合連続の利上げを決定】 
ニュージーランド準備銀行（RBNZ）は、９月１～２日に開催した定例理事会において、政策金利
であるオフィシャル・キャッシュ・レート （ＯＣＲ） を25bp 引き上げ、2.75％とすることを決定し
た（図１）。RBNZ では、2025 年 12 月にブレマン総裁が就任した。ブレマン体制下のRBNZ は、５月
の前回会合まで３会合連続で金利を据え置くなど、様子見姿勢を維持した。しかし、５月会合の据

### Page 2

2/4 
え置きについては、「３ （据え置き） 対３
（25bp の利上げ） 」と評決が割れたもの
の、決定権を有するブレマン氏が据え置
き票を投じて決定した。 その際に公表さ
れた声明文では、 先行きについて想定よ
り早く、 かつ大幅な利上げを行う必要性
が高まっているとの認識を示した。 そし
て、 ７月会合では約３年ぶりとなる利上
げを決定するなど金融引き締めに舵を
切った。このため、今回の利上げは２会
合連続となる。 
背景には、2025 年以降のインフレ率は加速しており、RBNZ が定める目標レンジ （２～３％） の上
限を上回る伸びで推移していることが挙げられる。 さらに、 イラン情勢の悪化を受けた原油高がエ
ネルギーインフレを招き、４-６月のインフレ率は前年比＋4.1％と 2023 年 10-12 月以来の高い伸
びに加速した（図２） 。 さらに、 この水準
は RBNZ が７月会合で示した見通し（前
年比＋3.9％） を上回り、 事前の市場予想
では RBNZ が追加利上げに動くとの見方
が広がった。 一方で、 コアインフレ率は
前年比＋2.5％と引き続き目標レンジの
範 囲 内 で 推 移 し て お り 、 前 期 （ 同 ＋
2.6％） からわずかに鈍化するなど、 エネ
ルギー以外の物価は 比較的 落ち着い て
いる。 価格転嫁の動きが広がっていない
ほか、 雇用不安が根強く賃金上昇圧力が
高まりにくいことも、 物価が上昇しにくい一因となっている可能性がある。このため、RBNZ が先行
きの政策運営に対してどのような見方を示すかが注目された。

【RBNZの先行きの利上げは緩やかに､総選挙を前に政治的な議論も活発化か】 
会合後に公表した声明文では、物価動向について 「イラン情勢による燃料価格の上昇で加速した」
とする一方、 「コアインフレ率、賃金上昇率、インフレ予想は一貫しており、2027 年半ばには目標
レンジに回帰し、年後半には中央値に収束する」との見通しを示した。一方で、景気動向について
は「不均一な状況が続いている」 として、 「海外の堅調な需要と輸出価格の上昇が所得と投資を押し
上げている」ものの、「雇用の不安定さ、 住宅価格の低迷が都市部 （オークランド・ウェリントン）
を中心とする家計消費と住宅投資の重しになっている」 との見方を示した（図３）。先行きの景気に
ついては「輸出は堅調に推移し、家計消費も徐々に増加するなど、回復の動きが広がりをみせる 」
図 2 インフレ率の推移

3/4 
との見方を示した。 そのうえで、「景気回
復とともに雇用環境も改善する」ほか、
「インフレ率が２％に収束するととも
に、家計部門の購買力も向上する」とし
た。 
世界経済については「商品価格や輸出
に影響を与えるリスクに直面している」
とした。 そして、 「同国の景気回復は想定
より力強いものとなる可能性もあれば、
弱いものになる可能性もある」として、
「インフレ圧力がより長期にわたる可能
性も残る」との見方を示した。そのため、先行きの政策運営について「引き続き警戒を怠らず、中
期的にインフレ率が２％に持続的に収束すべく、必要に応じて対応する」との考えを示した。 
今回の決定について「景気と雇用を支えつつ、インフレ率を２％に収束させるためには、金融引
き締めを段階的に解除することが適切と判断した」としている。一方で、 「今回の利上げ実施によ
り、将来、より大幅な利上げが必要になるリスクが低減される」 、 「今後の政策決定は、中期的なイ
ンフレへのリスクのバランスに関する判断に左右される」 として、 追加利上げに対して慎重姿勢を
強めている様子がうかがえる。 
会合後の記者会見に出席したブレマン総裁も、 先行きの政策運営について 「さらなる利上げを実
施する可能性は高いと考えており、 これまでの利上げの影響を評価する」 とした。 そして、 「想定し
ている政策金利の経路は５月会合時点で想定したものとほぼ同じ 」としつつ、 「重要なのは景気回
復の動きが想定通り広がりをみせるか否かである」 との見方を示した。 そのうえで、 「輸出セクター
は想定より著しく力強い動きが続いている」 とした。 ただし、 「予め決められた方針に固執せず、 利
上げのタイミングは極めて不確実である」 と述べるなど、これまでに比べて追加利上げへの慎重姿
勢が強まっている様子がうかがえる。そして、 「景気を下支えしつつインフレを抑えることができ
る」 、 「政策金利は中立方向に引き上げつつあるが、 依然として緩和的である」 としつつ、 「政策スタ
ンスを見極めるには多少の時間を要する可能性がある」との考えを示した。 
同国では 11 月に総選挙が予定されているが（注１）、ブレマン氏は「政策決定には影響しない」
との見方を示した。背景には、RBNZ の政策運営がここ数年政局争いに巻き込まれてきたことがあ
る。2023 年に発足したラクソン現政権は、 経済の再生と中間層の負担軽減を公約に掲げて誕生した
が、RBNZ による断続的な利上げ実施に伴う債務負担の増大は逆風となる可能性がある。 こうしたな
か、 最大野党の労働党は、 今回の決定に対して金利上昇が住宅ローンの返済負担増につながると主
張するなど、 総選挙を意識した 「場外戦」 を展開する動きをみせている。RBNZ は政策決定が総選挙

注１ ８月13 日付レポート「ラクソン政権に黄信号、ニュージーランド総選挙の行方」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/645257.html）

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用

に左右されない姿勢を明確にしており、先行きの利上げペースは、政治情勢そのものよりも、景気
回復の度合いやインフレ圧力の持続性を見極めつつ、 これまでより緩やかなものとなる可能性があ
る。

【RBNZが追加利上げの時期に慎重姿勢をみせるなか､NZドル相場を巡る環境は変化するか】 
このところの金融市場においては、日本と米国による協調介入に加え、 米国によるバイバック （長
期国債の買い入れ消却）の倍増表明を受け、その後の米ドル指数が軟調な動きを強めたため、ＮＺ
ドル相場は一時３ヵ月ぶりの高値となる
など強含みする動きをみせた （図４） 。 し
かし、足元ではＦＲＢ（米連邦準備制度
理事会）による利上げが意識されるなか
で一転して頭打ちしているほか、先行き
は RBNZ による利上げペースの鈍化が意
識されることで上値の重い展開が続く可
能性が高まっている。日本円に対しては、
円安が意識されるなかで強含みする展開
が続いてきたものの、ＮＺドル相場が上
値の重い展開をみせる可能性が高まって
いることを受けて、先行きは一転して調整圧力が強まる可能性に注意が必要であろう。

Document 376: Tokenised money and the future of FX (2026-09-10)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Tokenised money and the future of FX (2026-09-10).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-09-10
Pages: 28
Status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

Tokenised money  
and the future of FX

1 
Authors 
Rohini Grover, Ph.D. 
Strategist

10 September 2026 
Tokenisation has the potential to transform the way financial markets operate, from the 
movement of money and assets to the management of liquidity and risk. In this paper, we 
focus on tokenised forms of money and their impact on FX markets. 
Many emerging use cases for tokenised money are driven by a desire to reduce frictions 
associated with cross-border payments and FX settlement. Stablecoins enable 24/7 
cross-border transfers but remain overwhelmingly US dollar-denominated. As a result, 
they are currently more relevant as digital dollar infrastructure and for bridge-currency 
arrangements, often creating a "stablecoin sandwich" in which the US dollar serves as an 
intermediary between two currency legs, rather than providing a complete solution to the 
two-currency FX settlement problem 
Tokenisation may play a bigger role in transforming the combined securities and FX 
settlement cycles involved in cross-border capital flows. This is an area of increasing 
complexity, as delays or failures in one part of the transaction chain can affect the timely 
completion of the wider transaction, increasing operational, liquidity, and settlement 
risks. For example, following the transition to T+1 securities settlement in North America 
and with Europe preparing to move to T+1, the need to coordinate FX, cash, and securities 
settlement across institutions, jurisdictions, and infrastructures is already increasing. 
Tokenisation enables programmability, which allows atomic settlement and a range of 
operational and control functions across financial market infrastructures, including real-
time liquidity management, margin management, collateral mobilisation and substitution, 
and automated compliance checks. Current implementations have focused primarily on 
supporting corporate treasury and liquidity management activities involving FX. However, 
the settlement of tokenised securities and the associated FX transactions may offer even 
greater efficiencies by enabling synchronised settlement and reducing some of the 
operational, funding, and coordination frictions associated with shorter settlement cycles. 
Interoperability between different currencies, tokenised money arrangements, and 
payment systems remains one of the key challenges facing the development of tokenised 
cross-border payments and FX settlement. Several initiatives are exploring solutions 
involving tokenised central bank reserves or hybrid models that combine tokenised 
commercial bank money with central bank money. These may be particularly relevant for 
multi-currency settlement frameworks, preserving the two-tier monetary system while 
supporting settlement finality and more integrated cross-border processes. In parallel, 
other initiatives are building infrastructure that enables different forms of money, payment 
systems, and settlement architectures to operate together. 
Tokenisation promises substantial benefits but unintended consequences are worth 
considering. Beyond faster settlement, wholesale FX markets also depend on liquidity 
efficiency. This means banks and other financial institutions can settle large transaction 
volumes safely while using less liquidity. Existing infrastructures such as Continuous 
Linked Settlement (CLS) combine settlement risk mitigation with significant liquidity 
savings through multilateral netting and liquidity optimisation mechanisms. Unlike CLS, 
most current tokenised settlement models do not yet provide multilateral netting at

### Page 2

Deutsche Bank Research Institute 
Iran Shock: Key transmission channels – March 3, 2026

2

comparable scale. A number of initiatives are exploring liquidity-saving mechanisms and 
new approaches to liquidity management under tokenisation. 
We expect a period of accelerating change in years ahead. Securities and currencies 
settlement involves a complex nexus of technology, market infrastructure, regulation, and 
legal frameworks, necessitating cooperation between both public and private sector 
participants across jurisdictions.

Introduction
Tokenised money refers to digital representations of money that are recorded and
transferable on shared or distributed ledger infrastructure. This includes both state
and privately issued fiat-denominated forms of tokenised money, including
stablecoins, tokenised commercial bank deposits and tokenised central bank
money. 1 The potential role of tokenised money is increasingly being explored by

both corporates and institutional investors. Several use cases have emerged
ranging from cross-border payments, wholesale settlement, treasury and liquidity
management, trade finance and capital market infrastructure. More recently,
attention has expanded beyond using tokenised money for cross-border payments
towards models that can support the coordination and settlement of FX
transactions.
While tokenised money can support more efficient payments, FX settlement often

requires the conditional coordination of payments across currencies. Unlike a
simple payment, FX settlement requires the exchange of two currencies to occur
simultaneously, creating a need for a synchronisation or programmability layer
that enables Payment-versus-Payment (PvP) settlement and reduces settlement
risk. Tokenised money operating on programmable ledger-based infrastructure
can support this by embedding rules and conditions directly into settlement

processes, facilitating the settlement of both tokenised money and other tokenised
assets.
The settlement process, particularly  for wholesale FX transactions involving
financial institutions and large corporations, is exposed to settlement risk, often
referred to as Herstatt risk. This arises when one party delivers its currency but the
counterparty fails to fulfil its obligation. Cross-border transactions are particularly
vulnerable because time zone differences can create a window in which one side

has settled while the other has not. Over recent decades, this risk has been
materially reduced in major currencies through infrastructure such as CLS, helping
to support a large, liquid and operationally resilient FX market.
CLS mitigates FX settlement risk through PvP settlement, ensuring that one
currency is transferred only if the corresponding currency is delivered, while
reducing funding requirements via multilateral netting and liquidity optimisation

tools, generating liquidity savings of over 96% for settlement members. This
liquidity efficiency is important in wholesale markets because it determines the
amount of liquidity required for the system to settle its full volume of transactions
safely.
However, these benefits are not universal. Many emerging-market currencies
subject to current- or capital-account restrictions remain outside the CLS
ecosystem and continue to rely on correspondent banking networks or local

clearing systems. Settlement in these markets often involves bilateral pre-funding,
fragmented payment chains and complex liquidity management, creating
operational and funding frictions that can increase the cost and complexity of
cross-border transactions.
While settlement risk and liquidity frictions remain important challenges in FX
Wholesale: Refers to activity involving
banks, financial institutions, and large
corporates, rather than retail customers or

consumers.

### Page 5

FX Special Report
Deutsche Bank AG Page 5
markets, changes elsewhere in the financial system are creating new demands on
settlement infrastructure. As securities markets move from T+2 to T+1 settlement
and continue to explore even shorter settlement cycles, the movement of cash, FX,
securities and collateral has become increasingly interconnected. While existing
infrastructure continues to support these markets, cross-border funding and

settlement remain dependent on operating hours, time zones and multiple
intermediaries.
These developments have brought two related challenges into focus. The first is
improving cross-border settlement, particularly where correspondent banking and
non-CLS arrangements create liquidity and operational frictions. The second is
supporting a more integrated financial system in which cash, FX, securities and
collateral can move more seamlessly across institutions and jurisdictions.

Together, these challenges have strengthened interest in tokenised forms of money
as a potential means of modernising both payment and capital market
infrastructure.
The role of tokenised money in FX is closely linked to the broader tokenisation of
financial assets. As asset tokenisation gains momentum,  tokenised money is
increasingly viewed not merely as a payment instrument, but as an enabling layer
for more integrated settlement processes. The key question is therefore not

whether the technology is feasible, but whether it can achieve sufficient scale,
interoperability and integration with existing financial market infrastructures to
deliver meaningful improvements in cross-border settlement and capital market
efficiency.
Many tokenised money initiatives have focused on improving the cross-border
movement of money rather than directly settling FX trades. This reflects the scale
of corporate payment frictions:  global corporates move an estimated US$23.5

trillion across borders each year and remain heavily reliant on correspondent
banking networks, which generate approximately US$120 billion in annual cross-
border transaction fees. These figures exclude additional costs related to FX
conversion, trapped liquidity, pre-funding, settlement delays and operational
complexity, suggesting that the overall cost of cross-border treasury activity is
significantly higher. However, the scope of these initiatives is beginning to broaden.

As tokenised money infrastructure matures, some projects are moving beyond
payment efficiency towards models that could support FX settlement.
Against this backdrop, this paper examines the current FX settlement landscape
before assessing the role of tokenised money in FX. It evaluates the opportunities
and limitations of stablecoins, tokenised deposits and tokenised central bank
reserves, and considers whether these innovations can address challenges that

continue to characterise cross-border FX markets.
FX settlement today
FX trading takes place primarily in the over-the-counter (OTC) market, where banks,
dealers, electronic platforms and other market participants facilitate the exchange
of currencies. Once a trade is executed, it enters the post-trade process, during
which trade details are confirmed bilaterally or cleared through a central
counterparty (CCP), payment instructions are exchanged, and settlement

obligations are established (Figure 1). This process spans the period between trade
execution and final settlement, during which exposures are managed, liquidity is

### Page 6

10 September 2026
FX Special Report
Page 6 Deutsche Bank AG
arranged, and currencies are transferred between counterparties. Two types of
credit risk arise during this period: replacement cost risk, which exists from trade
execution until settlement, and principal risk, which arises during the settlement
process itself when one party may deliver the currency it sold but not receive the
currency it purchased. Depending on the currency pair, FX spot transactions

typically settle one or two business days after the trade date, while forwards and
options settle on their contractual maturity date, which may range from days to
several years after execution.
Figure 1: FX spot trading cycle
Source : Deutsche Bank
Figure 2: FX settlement via CLS
Source : Deutsche Bank, CLS
To reduce principal risk at settlement, often referred to as  settlement risk or
Herstatt risk, wholesale FX markets rely on infrastructure such as CLS, which

enables participating banks to settle eligible FX transactions on behalf of
themselves and their clients through a Payment-versus-Payment (PvP)
mechanism, ensuring that one currency is transferred only if the corresponding
currency is delivered (Figure 2).
How CLS works?
Today, CLS achieves simultaneous settlement through a defined funding and
settlement process rather than instantaneous settlement.  Under this process,
settlement is deferred to a daily settlement cycle, enabling multilateral netting

and liquidity optimisation before final settlement occurs. This is important in
wholesale markets because it determines the amount of liquidity required for the
system to settle its full volume of transactions safely. Settlement finality is
achieved through the use of central bank money.
n For FX trades eligible for settlement using CLS, settlement members or
their customers submit electronic payment instructions for both sides of
the transaction. CLS validates and matches these instructions, providing

legal confirmation and holding the transaction details until the settlement
date.
n On each settlement date, CLS simultaneously settles matched payment
instructions that satisfy its risk management requirements. Instead of every
FX trade being funded individually, CLS calculates each member's net
Gross versus net settlement: what does it
mean? Twenty currencies generate 190
unique bilateral currency pairs. Under
gross settlement, each currency leg must

be funded separately. Through multilateral
netting, these obligations are reduced to 20
net currency positions, one for each
currency, so participants fund only their net
obligations rather than every bilateral
payment.

### Page 7

10 September 2026
FX Special Report
Deutsche Bank AG Page 7
obligation across all eligible trades and currencies.
n Members then make funding payments and receive pay-outs at specified
times during the day. CLS holds accounts at the relevant central banks for
the currencies it settles. Settlement members pay and receive funds
through these CLS accounts via either their own central bank accounts or
correspondent banking (nostro) arrangements. Settlement members'

multicurrency accounts begin and end each business day with a zero
balance.
n The settlement of payment instructions and the associated payments
across the books of CLS Bank is final and irrevocable.
The PvP mechanism also illustrates that a form of atomic settlement already exists
in today's FX market. Once settlement occurs in CLS, it is final and irrevocable,
meaning the transfer of both currency legs cannot be unwound. However, as CLS
notes, atomic settlement can encompass both simultaneity and

instantaneousness, which are distinct concepts. Settlement can occur on an all-or-
nothing basis, with both currency legs settling simultaneously and with finality,
without necessarily taking place at the moment a trade is executed.
The CLS function differs from that of a central clearing counterparty (CCP). While
CLS's PvP mechanism eliminates FX settlement risk,  it does not eliminate
replacement cost ris k, which arises when a counterparty defaults before

settlement and the transaction must be replaced at prevailing market prices. These
risks can be particularly significant for FX forwards and swaps, where the period
between trade execution and final settlement may extend for months or even years.
CCPs such as ForexClear help mitigate counterparty credit and replacement cost
risk through central clearing and margining, whereas CLS is designed to address
settlement risk at the point of settlement by ensuring the simultaneous exchange

of both currency legs.
Settlement risks significantly reduced but not eliminated
According to the latest BIS Triennial Survey, banks and other financial institutions
account for approximately 95% of global FX turnover, compared with around 5% for
non-financial firms. Around 65% of financial-sector FX activity is cross-border in
nature. As a result, wholesale FX settlement is overwhelmingly driven by financial
institutions, underscoring the importance of robust cross-border settlement

infrastructure.
Although infrastructure such as CLS has significantly reduced settlement risk, its
coverage is not universal. CLS currently supports 18 currencies, and a significant
share of global FX activity continues to settle outside PvP arrangements. Results
from the BIS Triennial Survey show that just over US$5 trillion, representing 36% of
average daily FX settlement activity, was settled through PvP mechanisms that
eliminate settlement risk. A further US$7.6 trillion (54%) was settled through

arrangements such as pre-settlement netting that reduce, but do not eliminate,
settlement risk. Meanwhile, more than US$1.4 trillion (10%) of daily FX settlement
continued to occur on a gross bilateral basis through traditional correspondent
banking relationships, leaving participants fully exposed to settlement risk
(Figure 3).

that the two currency legs of an FX
transaction are settled simultaneously on a
payment-versus-payment (PvP) basis.
ForexClear mitigates counterparty credit
and replacement cost risk before
settlement through central clearing,
novation and margining. Replacement cost
risk arises after a trade is executed and

before it settles, when an adverse move in
market prices could leave one party
exposed if the counterparty defaults.

Notably, around 25% of this volume (US$1.4 trillion) was eligible for settlement

through a PvP system but was not settled in this way. Potential reasons include
operational challenges that result in trades missing PvP settlement cut-off times,
difficulties in managing credit exposures to correspondent banks that support PvP-
related payments, and the operational burden of meeting tight funding and
payment deadlines (Figure 4).
The remaining 75% of gross bilateral settlements were not eligible for PvP
settlement systems, primarily because one or both counterparties lacked direct or

indirect access to a PvP system. Other constraints included currency pairs that were
not eligible for PvP settlement, often involving emerging market currencies, as well
as trade types, such as certain same-day (T+0) transactions, that cannot currently
be settled through existing PvP arrangements. As a result, much of the residual
settlement risk reflects limitations in market access, currency coverage and
infrastructure capabilities rather than the voluntary non-use of PvP systems.

Given the dominance of financial institutions in FX trading, gross bilateral
settlements were also concentrated among financial counterparties. Of the US$1.4
trillion settled gross bilaterally, approximately 54% involved other financial
institutions, 33% involved reporting dealers, and 12% involved non-financial
institutions (Figure 4).
Digging deeper into trades within each counterparty category, PvP system to
mitigate risk is most common for transactions between reporting dealers or banks.

Only 11% of inter-dealer trade s are settled on a gross basis and exposed to
maximum risk. Consequently, FX trades most exposed to settlement risk are either
between reporting dealer and other financial institutions or reporting dealers and
non-financial institutions such as corporations. Around 19% of FX trades between
reporting dealers and other financial institutions are settled on a gross basis. As
non-bank financial institutions play an increasingly important role in cross-border

securities investment and funding activities, these exposures remain a relevant
source of risk outside the core PvP settlement infrastructure. Similarly, 22% of FX
trades between reporting dealers and non-financial institutions also settled
through gross bilateral settlement, exposing participants to the full principal
amount at risk.
